<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me examine this figure carefully. It's the overview Figure 1 for the paper with a three-panel layout:

**Left panel — Observation:**
- Shows a multi-turn conversation stack (System prompt, User turn 1, User turn 2, ... User turn N)
- GAR decay curve showing ↓70.8%
- Text about "Goal info persists in residual stream" with "AUC = 0.98 — available but attention bounds"

**Middle panel — Intervention: ThreadGate:**
- Shows the hypothesis: "A learnable gate should recover goal attention"
- Architecture diagram with formula x_{l+1} = x_l + g_l · f_l(x_l)


- The gate mechanism modulates the residual path using learned parameters and turn embeddings

**Right panel — Result & Root Cause:**
- Training improves GAR from 0.03 to 0.48, but same-distribution evaluation only reaches 0.49
- Model-generated text at inference drops to 0.016, revealing a 30x generalization gap
- The mechanism analysis traces this back to distribution shift caused by exposure bias

**Reviewing the presentation:**

The three-panel structure effectively guides readers through observation, intervention, and results. The key metrics are all present and the root cause is clearly marked. However, I'm noticing some readability concerns — the figure is relatively small at 1599×591 pixels, making the text quite cramped, potentially falling below conference standards for secondary annotations. The "same-dist eval" terminology could be clearer about whether this is teacher-forced evaluation. And the 30x generalization gap label appears to be cut off in the figure.

I need to clarify what the 30x gap actually represents — if training GAR is 0.48 and inference GAR drops to 0.016, that's the gap, but the same-distribution eval showing 0.49 suggests the distinction between teacher-forced and model-generated inference needs to be more explicit. I should also verify where the 70.8% decay and AUC=0.98 numbers come from, since the attention heatmap shows different decay values (0.875 and 0.777). The middle panel's gate mechanism formula looks correct, but I need to check what the turn_embed input represents.

For the right panel, the conclusion that distribution shift is the root cause while gradient competition isn't seems forward-looking — the PCGrad experiment was still running when this figure was made, yet the early v9 results already supported this direction, which aligns with Route B being the paper's chosen path. The orange-blue color scheme effectively highlights this key finding.

Now I'm checking the accuracy of the "GAR 0.03→0.48" claim against the actual data. The registry shows tg_ours_mid_gar at 0.5035 for held-out evaluation, but the training-time GAR would differ from inference GAR (which is 0.016 for model-generated outputs). The 0.03→0.48 range likely represents GAR-ours progressing from initialization to mid-training, though I need to verify this against the v7 logs. I'm realizing there's confusion about what "inference" means in the figure—it refers to model-generated text using the Dongre metric, not the teacher-forced held-out evaluation. The 30x gap is specifically about GAR-ours dropping from ~0.48 during training to ~0.016 at inference, which are two different evaluation setups. I'm realizing the 30x gap is likely between teacher-forced evaluation contexts where GAR-ours stays around 0.50, versus model-generated text where the metric drops significantly—so it's a distribution shift problem, not a comparison between different metric definitions. So the 0.016 likely comes from a separate inference-mode evaluation where the model generates its own responses across multiple turns, rather than the teacher-forced held-out eval. The key issue is whether the figure's numbers are accurate and properly labeled, and I should flag concerns about font size readability, the vague "Same-dist eval" label, and whether the mechanism analysis conclusion about gradient competition is premature given that v9 is still running.
```

[warning] \"Same-dist eval: GAR = 0.49\" 标签不够清晰**\n\"Same-dist\" 是项目内部术语。审稿人不会直接理解。建议改为 \"Teacher-forced eval: GAR = 0.49\" 或 \"Eval (gold context): 0.49\"。这也帮助读者理解 30x gap 的来源——teacher-forced context vs model-generated context 的对比。\n\n**3. [warning] 数字溯源需确认**\n- \"GAR 0.03→0.48\"：0.03 的来源？Registry 中 base_ours_mid_gar = 0.013。如果是 training 初始 GAR，需要确认具体数据来源。\n- \"↓70.8%\"：来自哪个分析？Registry attention_heatmap 中 base_decay=0.875, tg_decay=0.777。70.8% 不直接对应这些数字，需要标注数据来源。\n- \"AUC = 0.98\"：来自残差流分析，但 registry 中未见此数字。需确认出处。\n- \"GAR = 0.016\"：对应 registry tg_dongre_mid_gar = 0.0152（四舍五入 OK）。但如果 \"Training GAR\" 用的是 GAR-ours 而 \"Inference GAR\" 用的是 GAR-Dongre，这是两个不同指标的比较——审稿人会攻击这一点。需确认 30x gap 是同一指标（GAR-ours 或 GAR-Dongre）在不同分布下的对比。\n\n**4. [warning] 右栏 \"Gradient competition: NOT root cause\" 结论时机**\nv9 PCGrad 还在训练中（~5% 进度），inference eval 尚未完成。虽然早期数据（cos_sim 转正但 L_GAR 仍坍塌）强烈支持此结论，但将\"未完成实验的结论\"放入论文 Figure 1 需要在实验完成后做最终确认。当前版本可以保留作为草稿，但需标记为 \"pending v9 completion\"。\n\n### 建议\n- 修正字号（主 ≥6pt，次 ≥5pt）\n- \"Same-dist\" → \"Teacher-forced eval\" 或等价的读者友好标签\n- 交叉核实所有数字的 registry 出处，确保 30x gap 用的是同一 GAR 指标\n- v9 完成后确认右栏因果排除结论",
  "project": "turn_residual_gating"
}

```text
Let me analyze the ROADMAP update:

1. **v9 PCGrad status**: ROADMAP says "v9完成+派eval"但registry仍是DONE/failed，GPU显示PID 83865还在运行（86464 MiB）。这有矛盾——ROADMAP说v9完成了但GPU还有进程在跑。这可能是v9 eval正在运行（not the training）。Let me think: ROADMAP says "v9 eval 运行中" and GPU shows 86GB usage. If v9 training completed, the eval would be a different process but might use similar memory. 

Looking at the ROADMAP more carefully, it shows v9 training is done and v9 eval is now running, with the queue showing v9 eval → v8b (8h) → seed (6h) in serial. But the registry still marks v9 as DONE/failed, though the notes mention the 3rd submission is running normally. There's a disconnect between what the ROADMAP claims (v9 completed) and what the registry shows (failed status), even though the notes suggest the current process is running fine.

The memory usage jumped from 52.7GB to ~84GB, which aligns with eval typically requiring more memory than training for full attention matrices, so the current GPU process is likely the v9 eval. I should verify what's actually executing though.

Looking at the ROADMAP content, the core direction now includes PCGrad causal exclusion, and D028 correctly documents the gradient direction conflict with the cos_sim improvement of +0.086 matching the step 200 data. The v9 PCGrad registry still shows DONE/failed status despite the notes indicating normal execution.

The GPU plan looks reasonable with v9 eval running followed by v8b and seed runs totaling 14 hours serially, and v8b code is already reviewed and ready. The GPU expansion request is blocked on three unanswered manager requests, which is the primary blocker for Llama-3. Paper Agent work is progressing with Fig1 reviewed and Step 2 plot generation underway.

There's a discrepancy in the ROADMAP labeling though — it lists the allocated GPU as RTX 4090, but the actual hardware is RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell with 102GB, not 24GB. The 86GB usage confirms it's the Blackwell in use, so this is just a stale label issue in the documentation. The cumulative GPU time of ~50 hours for training plus ~5 hours for eval seems high for a single day, so I'm checking the breakdown across v7, v8, and v9 runs.

The key issues I need to focus on are the registry inconsistency that's been flagged before, the GPU label mismatch, whether v9 training actually completed successfully, and whether the overall plan is realistic. Let me verify the v9 training completion status.
```

_source line 1602 · 2026-05-19T20:00:48.440Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me examine Figure 2 carefully.

**Panel (a) — "Three-phase convergence to target":**
- Y-axis: "Training GAR" (range 0–0.5+)
- X-axis: "Global Step" (range 0–200+)
- Shows GAR trajectory with three phases labeled A, B, and colored regions
- Phase A: GAR stays near 0 → ~0.05 (green region, up to ~gs 75)
- Phase B: Rapid rise from ~0.05 to ~0.50 (orange region, gs 75–144)


- The transition happens at step 75, with the target of 0.5 reached by step 144, after which GAR fluctuates around that target level. The background shows the normal task loss declining from 2.03 to 0.93 across the same training window, with the data sourced from v7 micro-step logs converted to global steps.

**Panel (b) — "PCGrad delays but cannot prevent collapse":**
- Y-axis: "L_GAR (MSE loss)" (range 0–0.25)
- X-axis: "Global Step" (range 0–1100)
- Two curves comparing training dynamics...

Writing comparison analysis... the formula actually holds up well across the range—it correctly reconstructs GAR values from zero through the target, and while it might underestimate slightly when GAR exceeds 0.5, that error is negligible since the loss is so small near the target anyway.

But there's a mathematical subtlety I'm noticing: since L_GAR is defined as (GAR - 0.5)², taking the square root gives |GAR - 0.5|, which means the reconstruction formula GAR = 0.5 - √L_GAR actually inverts values above the target—it would give 1 - GAR instead of GAR itself. In practice though, GAR tends to overshoot briefly before settling back, so when it spikes above 0.5 around step 144, the plot shows those values reflected around the midpoint rather than at their true positions. The formula discrepancy is a real concern—either the stated formula differs from what's actually plotted, or there's an issue with how the GAR values are being computed or displayed. For panel (b), the x-axis extends to 1100 global steps, showing v7 collapsing early and v9 plateauing around step 325 before its own collapse, though the training data I saw earlier only went to step 769, so there may be extrapolation or newer data I'm not accounting for.

The worker's annotation of "≈325 steps delay" aligns reasonably with my earlier estimate of ~350 steps—just different ways of pinpointing the collapse point. The core finding that direction conflict is eliminated but L_GAR still collapses is the key insight and checks out against the PCGRAD results. Panel (a) smartly isolates v7's first epoch (200 global steps) where the interesting GAR dynamics occur, since the metric stabilizes near target afterward in teacher-forced training. The typography and color choices look cleaner than the previous figure, with blue for v7 and presumably other colors for the remaining variants.

The NTP loss annotation showing convergence from ~2.0 to ~0.93 matches what I see in the gray background curve. I had two concerns about the figure—the back-calculation formula potentially creating artifacts when GAR overshoots 0.5, and whether v9's data in panel (b) is complete—but the second one resolves itself since the figure only extends to step 1100, which is already past the collapse point, so the rest of the training would just show L_GAR flatlined near zero and add nothing visually.

For the first concern though, I'm noticing those oscillations in panel (a) do push GAR above 0.5, which shouldn't be possible if it's computed as 0.5 - √L_GAR. Either the worker used a different formula with sign detection, logged GAR directly instead of deriving it from L_GAR, or the figure script has additional logic I'm not seeing. I should check the actual figure script to understand what's happening.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the `turn_residual_gating` ML research project targeting EMNLP 2026 (ARR August cycle). My role is to independently verify experiment conclusions, check data integrity, review ROADMAP/framing decisions, and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I do NOT execute experiments or modify files — only read-only access. All output must be in Chinese. This session continued from two prior context-compacted sessions. The main work involved: reviewing multiple worker outputs (fix workers, code workers, analysis workers, figure workers), monitoring v9 PCGrad training progress, catching ROADMAP errors, evaluating paper claim implications of PCGrad data, and discovering a critical x-axis scale bug in Figure 2.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **ThreadGate architecture**: `x_{l+1} = x_l + g_l · f_l(x_l)` (ReZero-style, gate on sub-layer output)
   - **GAR (Goal Attention Ratio)**: GAR-ours (last token → system prompt + first user query) and GAR-Dongre (all response tokens → task tokens). 30x gap is distribution shift specific to teacher-forced vs model-generated contexts.
   - **Route B**: Negative result + mechanistic insight paper — lightweight residual gating fails due to teacher-forcing→generation distribution shift (exposure bias).
   - **PCGrad (Yu et al., NeurIPS 2020)**: Projects g_ntp onto g_gar-orthogonal plane when cos_sim < 0. Only applied to gate parameters. Two backward passes with retain_graph=True. Reported cos_sim is PRE-projection; applied gradients have cos_sim ≥ 0 by construction.
   - **D026 SS Bug**: `compute_gar_loss()` returns 0.0 when `len(gar_per_turn) < 2`. SS generates only 1 assistant turn → SS path never produces GAR gradient signal.
   - **v8b Fix**: Added len==1 branch computing `(gar_per_turn[0] - target)**2` directly; eval()+no_grad() for generation; lambda_gar corrected to 1.0.
   - **PCGrad Causal Exclusion (D028)**: cos_sim oscillates around zero (sometimes positive +0.086) from step 200+, but L_GAR collapses identically. Direction conflict is causally excluded as primary mechanism. Magnitude dominance (3-9x) persists but is also uncorrelated with L_GAR trajectory.
   - **Figure 2 x-axis bug**: v7 data properly converts micro-steps to global steps (/8), but v9 data uses raw micro-steps labeled as global steps. The "≈325 steps delay" annotation is wrong by ~8x factor.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/turn_residual_gating/src/train_phase2_v9_pcgrad.py`** (remote server)
     - PCGrad training script, always-on PCGrad mode (no --pcgrad flag needed)
     - PCGRAD diagnostics at `global_step % 50 == 0` (line ~442-460)
     - Training metrics at `step % 50 == 0` where `step` is micro-step (line ~471)
     - 15 PCGRAD diagnostic points collected (step 50-750): cos_sim oscillates [-0.095, +0.090], mag_ratio 2.6-13.7x
     - Training at Epoch 0 Step 6150/24000 (25.6%), L_GAR collapsed to 0.0004, Gate mean 0.966

   - **`/root/turn_residual_gating/src/train_phase2_v8b.py`** (remote server, newly created)
     - D026 fix at L401-402: `if len(gar_per_turn) == 1 and args.gar_loss_mode == "mse_target": l_gar = (gar_per_turn[0] - args.gar_target) ** 2`
     - OOM fix: `remove_gate_hooks(model)` → `model.base_model.eval()` + `torch.no_grad()` → generation → `model.base_model.train()` + `restore_gate_hooks(model, transformer_layers)` + `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` in try/finally
     - `restore_gate_hooks` (L109-114): complete implementation using `model._make_hook(i)`
     - `transformer_layers` defined at L197: `list(model.base_model.model.layers)`
     - SS scheduling at L320-322: `ss_ratio = min(effective_step / effective_total, args.ss_max_ratio)` with ss_warmup_steps=0
     - SS/TF separate tracking: `ss_gar_sum/count`, `tf_gar_sum/count` (L286-289, L513-521)
     - Reviewed and PASSED

   - **`/root/turn_residual_gating/scripts/run_v8b.sh`** (remote)
     - Correct hyperparams: `--lambda-gar 1.0 --init-bias 1.5 --gate-lr-multiplier 10 --gar-target 0.5 --min-gate-std 0.05 --ss-gen-max-tokens 128`

   - **`figures/paper/gen_fig2.py`** (local workspace)
     - **CRITICAL BUG**: v7 data correctly converted: `v7_gs = v7_ms / 8.0` (line 36). v9 data NOT converted: `v9_gs = np.array([0, 50, 100, ...1100])` (line 56-58) — these are micro-steps labeled as global steps.
     - v7 L_GAR data at lines 38-43, v9 L_GAR data at lines 60-63
     - Panel (a) uses `v7_gar = 0.5 - np.sqrt(v7_lgar)` (line 44) — valid for GAR ≤ 0.5
     - "≈325 steps delay" annotation is wrong due to scale mismatch

   - **`figures/paper/fig1_overview.png`** — Three-panel overview figure (Observation → Intervention → Result). Warnings: 4.5pt font too small, "Same-dist eval" unclear, number provenance needs verification.

   - **`figures/paper/fig2_training_pcgrad.png`** — Two-panel figure: (a) Three-phase GAR convergence, (b) v7 vs v9 L_GAR. CRITICAL x-axis scale bug.

   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: v9 PCGrad still shows status=DONE, conclusion=failed with stale notes about --pcgrad flag error. Reported 3 times, still not fixed.

   - **ROADMAP.md**: Corrected after my CRITICAL report — v9 status, GPU label (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell not RTX 4090), GPU plan (20.7h not 14h).

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **ssh_read_only metacharacter blocking (`|`)**: grep patterns with `|` alternation blocked by security policy. Fixed by using separate grep calls with single patterns.
   - **ROADMAP "v9完成" wrong (CRITICAL, POSTED)**: ROADMAP claimed v9 completed when training was at 25.6%. I verified via `ps -p 83865` showing the training process still running for 02:03:05 and log at Step 6150/24000. Director corrected ROADMAP.
   - **GPU label wrong**: ROADMAP said "RTX 4090" but actual GPU is "NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition" (102GB). Corrected after my report.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **v8b code review**: Verified all three D026 fixes (len==1 guard, OOM prevention, lambda_gar confound). Confirmed hyperparams aligned with v7/v9. Confirmed SS scheduling logic, SS/TF separate tracking, restore_gate_hooks completeness. PASSED.
   - **PCGrad causal analysis**: Assembled complete 15-point PCGRAD dataset showing cos_sim oscillates around zero from step 200+ while L_GAR collapses regardless. Concluded directional conflict is causally excluded. Analyzed implications for C3 claim, D027 conclusion, §6.1 positioning, and necessity of inference GAR eval.
   - **v9 training monitoring**: Tracked from micro_step 100 through 6150. L_GAR collapsed at step 1150 (0.0007). Gate reached 0.966 with some layers at max 1.000.

        "content": "{\n  \"raw\": \"日志共 53 行，训练仍在 Epoch 0 step 1100，且 PCGRAD 诊断仅 2 个采样点（step 50 和 100）。以下是完整分析。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## PCGRAD 诊断汇总\\n\\n| step | cos_sim | mag_ratio_pre | mag_ratio_post | conflict_layers | conflicting_steps |\\n|------|---------|---------------|----------------|-----------------|-------------------|\\n| 50   | -0.0610 | 2.81          | 2.78           | 54.0%           | 4/8               |\\n| 100  | -0.0707 | 6.01          | 5.97           | 57.6%           | 7/8               |\\n\\n## L_GAR 趋势（每50步）\\n\\n| step | L_GAR (v9) | vs v7 同阶段 |\\n|------|-----------|-------------|\\n| 0    | 0.2342    | ~0.23 (相同起点) |\\n| 50   | 0.2349    | — |\\n| 100  | 0.2337    | — |\\n| 150  | 0.2270    | — |\\n| 200  | 0.2339    | — |\\n| 250  | 0.2280    | — |\\n| 300  | 0.2205    | — |\\n| 350  | 0.2196    | — |\\n| 400  | 0.2090    | v7 已开始快速下降 |\\n| 450  | 0.1675    | — |\\n| 500  | 0.1760    | — |\\n| 550  | 0.1673    | — |\\n| 600  | 0.1244    | — |\\n| 650  | 0.1102    | — |\\n| 700  | 0.0939    | — |\\n| 750  | 0.0737    | — |\\n| **800** | **0.0832** | **v7 ≈ 0.006** |\\n| 850  | 0.0491    | — |\\n| 900  | 0.0410    | — |\\n| 950  | 0.0304    | — |\\n| 1000 | 0.0343    | — |\\n| 1050 | 0.0239    | — |\\n| 1100 | 0.0367    | — |\\n\\n## Gate 趋势\\n\\n| step | mean   | std    | min    | max    |\\n|------|--------|--------|--------|--------|\\n| 0    | 0.8097 | 0.0341 | 0.6836 | 0.8672 |\\n| 50   | 0.8101 | 0.0572 | 0.7109 | 0.9453 |\\n| 100  | 0.8717 | 0.0497 | 0.7773 | 0.9766 |\\n\\n训练行中 Gate 值的趋势：0.810 → 0.816 → 0.827 → 0.856 → 0.869 → 0.882 → 0.877 → **0.899** (step 1100)\\n\\n## 分析结论\\n\\n### 1. PCGrad 投影基本无效\\n\\n- **cos_sim 仍为负值**：step 50 = -0.061，step 100 = -0.071。v7 epoch 0 的 cos_sim = -0.089。PCGrad 后 cos_sim 没有变正，说明投影并未消除梯度冲突方向——它只在逐层投影，但聚合后全局冲突仍然存在。\\n- **mag_ratio 几乎不变**：pre→post 变化仅 ~1%（2.81→2.78, 6.01→5.97）。NTP 梯度仍以 3-6x 的幅度压制 GAR。\\n- **conflict_layers 在上升**：54% → 57.6%，且 conflicting_steps 从 4/8 → 7/8，说明随着训练推进冲突变得更普遍。\\n\\n### 2. L_GAR 衰减被减缓但未被阻止\\n\\n- **step 800 对比**：v9 L_GAR = 0.083 vs v7 = 0.006，v9 高 **13.8x**。PCGrad 确实让 L_GAR 在前期衰减更慢。\\n- **但 L_GAR 仍在单调下降**：0.234 → 0.037 (step 1050)，下降了 **84%**。趋势上看正在趋近 0，只是速度比 v7 慢约 2-3x。\\n- **step 0-350 是\\\"高原期\\\"**（0.23→0.22），之后加速下降。v7 没有这个高原期。\\n- 结论：PCGrad 延缓了坍塌但无法阻止。到 epoch 1-2，L_GAR 大概率也会趋近 0。\\n\\n### 3. NTP 仍然主导\\n\\n- mag_ratio 从 2.81（step 50）跃升到 6.01（step 100），趋势与 v7（epoch 0: 7.5 → epoch 2: 14.7）一致。NTP 梯度在训练中持续放大其相对优势。\\n- Gate 持续走高（0.81 → 0.90），与 v7 行为一致：模型通过关闭 turn 分支来降低 Loss。\\n\\n### 4. 对 paper 因果论证的意义\\n\\n这组结果**强化因果链**：\\n\\n> **梯度冲突 → NTP 主导 → gate 坍塌 → L_GAR 消失**\\n\\nPCGrad 是一个\\\"弱干预\\\"——它投影掉逐层冲突分量但保留了量级不对称。结果符合预期：\\n- 减缓但不阻止坍塌 → 证明梯度冲突是**必要条件之一**但非唯一原因\\n- mag_ratio 不变 → **量级主导**(magnitude dominance) 是独立于方向冲突的另一致因\\n- 这为 paper 提供了 ablation 证据：方向投影 alone 不够，还需要量级均衡（如 GradNorm/动态权重）\\n\\n**注意**：PCGRAD 诊断仅 2 个采样点，且训练还在 Epoch 0。需要等完整 3 epoch 训练结束后才能下最终结论。目前的趋势已经足够说明 PCGrad 的局限性。\"\n}",
        "numLines": 3,

        "totalLines": 3
      }

Panel (b) 两条曲线 x 轴尺度不同：v7 范围 gs 0–200，v9 范围实际是 ms 0–1100（应为 gs 0–137.5）\n2. \"≈325 steps delay\" 标注（L149）完全错误——基于不可比的两个尺度计算，实际延迟需要在统一尺度下重新测量\n3. 若 v9 也除以 8，v9 数据范围仅 gs 0–137.5，比 v7 的 gs 0–200 还短，delay 值和视觉呈现会完全不同\n\n## 修复\n\n1. 确认 v9 训练日志中 `step` 字段的含义（micro-step 还是 global step）\n2. 如果是 micro-step（大概率），加 `v9_gs = v9_gs / 8.0`\n3. 重新计算实际 delay 并更新标注\n4. 重新生成 PDF/PNG\n\n## 来源\n\nv9 分析 worker 结果（worker_analysis_v9_pcgrad_cossim_check）中 L_GAR 表格标注为\"每50步\"，对应 v9 训练脚本中 `step % 50 == 0` 的 micro-step 级输出。v7 日志同样是 micro-step 级（ms 0–1600，/8 → gs 0–200）。两者数据源都是 micro-step，但 gen_fig2.py 只对 v7 做了转换。",

1. **PCGRAD diagnostics** (line 442): logged at `global_step % 50 == 0` — these ARE global steps
2. **Training metrics** (line 471): logged at `step % 50 == 0` — `step` is the micro-step (loop variable)
3. **`global_step` increment** (line 439): `global_step += 1` happens after every `grad_accum` micro-steps

So the v9 analysis worker's L_GAR table (step 0, 50, 100, ...1100) — these values come from line 471's `step % 50 == 0` output which is the MICRO-step. 

But wait, the PCGRAD diagnostics at line 442 are logged at global step intervals, not micro-steps. Let me look at what the analysis worker actually extracted from the training log more carefully. The L_GAR table shows 23 data points spanning from step 0 to 1100, and I need to trace back whether these came from the micro-step logging or the global step logging. Looking at gen_fig2.py to see how it's processing the logged values...

Defining the step array... With `grad_accum = 8`, micro-step 50 corresponds to global step 6.25, and micro-step 1100 to global step 137.5. The training loop increments `step` by 1 for each micro-batch while `global_step` increments every 8 micro-steps, so PCGRAD diagnostics log at global steps 50, 100, 150, and training metrics log at micro-steps 0, 50, 100, 150.

Looking at the actual logged data, the PCGRAD table has only 2 entries (global steps 50 and 100), but the L_GAR table has 23 entries spanning micro-steps 0 through 1100. This suggests L_GAR was logged at micro-step intervals while PCGRAD was logged at global-step intervals, which would mean the training reached micro-step 1100 but only global step ~137.5.

This confirms that the `v9_gs` values in gen_fig2.py are actually micro-steps, not global steps as the variable name suggests — they need to be divided by 8 to convert to global steps.

Now I need to verify whether v7 used the same logging granularity.

Looking at the v7 data, it correctly labels the values as micro-steps and performs the conversion, so v9 should follow the same pattern.

I've already posted my critical report about this issue. Let me check the current training status of v9 to see how much progress has been made, and also verify whether the more recent analysis data from step 6150 has the same problem.

NeurIPS 2020) 消除 NTP-GAR 梯度方向冲突后（仅对 gate 参数投影 NTP 梯度到 GAR 梯度正交平面），如果 30x training-inference GAR gap 仍在 → 因果证据证明 exposure bias（非梯度竞争）是 gap 根因。与 v7 对比：相同超参（lambda_gar=1.0, MSE+gate_div），唯一变量是梯度处理方式。成功标准：(1) 梯度 cos_sim 显著改善（从 v7 的 -0.089 提升到 ≥0），(2) 但推理 GAR 仍 ≈ v7 水平（0.016），证明梯度冲突不是 gap 的因。\", \"parent\": \"phase2_tg_s42_v7\", \"spec\": {\"command\": \"bash -c \\\"source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/turn_residual_gating && PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 python src/train_phase2_v9_pcgrad.py --model /root/.cache/modelscope/Qwen/Qwen2___5-7B-Instruct --data-dir data/ultrachat_8k --output-dir checkpoints/tg_v9_pcgrad_s42 --gate-type threadgate --gar-loss-mode mse_target --gar-target 0.5 --lambda-gar 1.0 --lambda-gate-div 1.0 --init-bias 1.5 --gate-lr-multiplier 10 --lr 2e-4 --epochs 3 --batch-size 1 --grad-accum 8 --seed 42\\\"\", \"server\": \"westd-20861\", \"gpu\": [\"cuda:0\"], \"pid\": \"83860\", \"log_path\": \"~/runs/turn_residual_gating/phase2_tg_s42_v9_pcgrad.log\", \"submitted_at\": \"2026-05-19T17:42:10.820Z\", \"submitted_via\": \"submit_training_job\", \"backend\": \"tmux\", \"account\": \"root\"}, \"reproduce\": {\"finished_at\": \"2026-05-19T17:35:37.703Z\"}, \"failure_reason\": null, \"conclusion\": null, \"extra\": {\"hook_events\": [{\"event\": \"on_failed\", \"action\": \"agent\", \"ts\": \"2026-05-19T17:35:39.776243+00:00\", \"note\": \"第1次提交 EXIT_CODE=2 残留事件\"}]}, \"completed_at\": null, \"notes\": \"第3次提交 (PID 83860, westd-20861:cuda:0)。前两次失败：#1 EXIT_CODE=2 (--pcgrad flag 不存在), #2 EXIT_CODE=143 (服务器重启 SIGTERM at step 100)。第3次运行正常，Epoch 0 step 6150/24000 micro-steps (25.6%)。超参与 v7 一致。训练端发现：L_GAR 在 step 0-350 有高原期 (0.22-0.23)，之后缓慢下降，step 5000+ 坍塌到≈0（同 v7 终态）。PCGrad cos_sim 从一致负值演变为围绕 0 振荡，但 NTP mag_ratio 5-10x 主导不变。\"}}, \"count\": 1, \"total\": 1, \"baseline\": null}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.0625}}"}

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. 35	def rbox(x, y, w, h, fc='white', ec='#BBB', lw=0.6, zorder=2, pad=0.1):
36	    p = FancyBboxPatch((x, y), w, h, boxstyle=f'round,pad={pad}',
37	                        facecolor=fc, edgecolor=ec, linewidth=lw, zorder=zorder)
38	    ax.add_patch(p)
39	
40	def arr(x1, y1, x2, y2, c='#444', lw=0.9, ls='-', rad=0.0, ms=7,
41	        shrA=1, shrB=1):
42	    a = FancyArrowPatch(
43	        (x1, y1), (x2, y2),
44	        arrowstyle=f'-|>,head_length={ms*0.15},head_width={ms*0.08}',

50	def txt(x, y, s, fs=7, c=C_TXT, ha='center', va='center', fw='normal', **kw):
51	    return ax.text(x, y, s, fontsize=fs, color=c, ha=ha, va=va,
52	                   fontweight=fw, zorder=5, **kw)

Let me carefully review Figure 3. I need to verify:

1. **Data accuracy**: Are the numbers in the figure consistent with registry data?
2. **Visual quality**: Font sizes ≥ 7pt, clear labeling, etc.
3. **Scientific correctness**: Do the claims/annotations match the evidence?

**Panel (a): Gate Turn Conditioning**

Data claims:
- Top-5 layers with largest gate shift: L9, L20, L12, L14, L23
- Turn 1 values: 0.8578, 0.8228, 0.8200, 0.7473, 0.7693


- Turn 21 values: 0.9679, 0.9393, 0.9465, 0.9276, 0.9793

The differences between early and late turns are substantial—ranging from 0.11 to 0.21—and the Wilcoxon test shows this pattern is highly significant (p = 3.73×10⁻⁸, r = 0.975). I'm spot-checking the arithmetic on a few of these differences and they're checking out. The broader finding that 27 out of 28 layers show increased gating at later turns is a strong signal that the model systematically conditions its behavior based on conversation progression.

Now looking at panel (b) on distribution shift. The Base model shows minimal difference between IID and model-generated data (0.0107 vs 0.0122), but the TG model exhibits a dramatic gap (0.4866 vs 0.0157), suggesting it's highly sensitive to whether inputs come from the training distribution or the model itself. The UG model shows similar sensitivity to TG on the IID side but no model-generated data available.

I'm flagging a potential issue: the Base model's GAR actually increases slightly on model-generated text rather than decreasing, which is counterintuitive given exposure bias concerns. However, this makes sense because the base model lacks a gate mechanism, so it has no learned bias to exploit. The difference is marginal anyway and likely noise.

The UG and LoRA-only evaluations for model-generated text are marked as "n/a" since they haven't been run yet—this is transparent but might prompt reviewer questions about completeness. The log scale on panel (b) makes the IID values for TG, UG, and LoRA-only cluster together visually, but since the 30× gap is the key finding, this presentation works. Font sizes all meet the 7pt minimum requirement. The "n/a" labels are positioned appropriately on the plot.

Now I'm checking the bar value label formatting—the code uses three decimal places for values below 0.1 and two decimal places otherwise, so 0.0107 displays as "0.011" and 0.4866 displays as "0.49". There's some rounding happening here, but it's consistent with the formatting logic. The values are close enough that rounding to two decimal places makes them indistinguishable on the chart, but they're actually slightly different—TG is 0.4866, UG is 0.4923, and LoRA-only is 0.486. For the mg values, I'm checking what precision level makes sense with four decimal places.

The "30×" annotation sits at the geometric mean between TG's IID and model-gen values on the log scale, which works out to around 0.0874. The key claim in the title is that exposure bias selectively disrupts GAR only in the gated model—Base shows minimal change from 0.0107 to 0.0122, while TG drops dramatically from 0.4866 to 0.0157, which clearly supports that selective disruption.

For the first subplot, all five shown layers have higher gate values at Turn 21 compared to Turn 1, indicating they become more passive over time. This pattern holds across 27 of the 28 total layers. Now I need to cross-check these numbers against the actual registry data to make sure everything aligns—specifically the `eval_gate_dynamic_range_top5` field from the phase 2 TG evaluation and the 4-way comparison metrics. I'm also noticing that TG's model-gen GAR at 0.0157 is actually slightly higher than Base's 0.0122, which is an interesting detail.

```

The Figure 3 code is plotting these same five layers with their turn1 values in the specified order.

Cross-checking all the values against the registry confirms everything matches perfectly — L9, L20, L12, L14, and L23 all line up with their respective metrics.

Now I'm looking at the distribution shift metrics for the four-model panel, pulling from the phase2_4way_eval registry. I'm verifying the model-generation metrics match up correctly—the baseline and TG values align with the registry data, while UG and LoRA weren't evaluated. The Wilcoxon statistic of p = 3.73×10⁻⁸ with r = 0.975 makes sense given that 27 out of 28 layers show consistent gate increases at late turns. The 30× multiplier checks out: 0.4866 divided by 0.0157 gives roughly 31×.

Now I need to clarify what "IID eval" versus "model-generated" actually means in the context of Panel (b)—the IID evaluation appears to be teacher-forced on the ultrachat eval split, while model-generated refers to the v7 inference evaluation where the model generates its own context. Let me trace through the registry to confirm: the `phase2_4way_eval` shows teacher-forced GAR measurements on the eval data with Base at 0.0107 and TG at 0.4866, which should correspond to the IID baseline in the panel.

The model-generated evaluation from `phase2_tg_s42_v7` gives an eval GAR of 0.0157 for TG, which explains the roughly 30× gap between 0.4866 and 0.0157. This isn't really IID versus OOD in the traditional sense—it's the distribution shift that occurs when the model generates its own text rather than being teacher-forced on the training distribution, and the figure's labeling captures this distinction accurately.

I'm checking whether the figure should use the held-out evaluation data instead of the original eval split. The held-out metrics show similar results (TG at 0.5035 versus 0.4866 in the original), with only a 0.75 percentage point spread, so using the original eval data is fine—the held-out validation confirms the same pattern holds. The held-out evaluation used completely separate data with no training overlap and confirmed the same pattern, so the potential data leakage concern doesn't affect the conclusions. The model-generated metrics only come from the original evaluation since the held-out set only includes teacher-forced IID scores, which is fine since model-generated data doesn't have overlap issues anyway. Now I'm verifying the final checklist — all registry values match, the arithmetic checks out, typography meets standards, and the labels are clear. The model-generated results reveal the core issue: ThreadGate achieves high GAR under teacher-forced conditions (0.4866) but this collapses dramatically when the model generates its own context (0.0157), demonstrating classic exposure bias. Interestingly, ThreadGate still edges out the base model slightly in the model-generated setting (0.0157 vs 0.0122, a 28.7% relative improvement), but this is negligible compared to the massive 30× gap in the IID setting—the distribution shift essentially erases the learned behavior.

I'm noticing that the bar chart shows model-generated evaluations only for Base and ThreadGate, while UG and LoRA-only are marked as "n/a"—this missing data will likely prompt reviewer questions about whether those methods also collapse similarly or show different patterns. The 4-way comparison notes mention that all three training methods converge at inference time with ~47-48pp GAR recovery, but that's the IID eval; the model-generated eval was only run for ThreadGate and Base in v7, so I need to check the registry to confirm what's actually been evaluated. ```text
1	"""Generate Figure 4: Goal Attention Decay — Gating adds zero inference overhead."""
2	import matplotlib
3	matplotlib.use('Agg')
4	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
5	import matplotlib.patheffects as pe
6	import numpy as np
7	import os
8	
9	plt.rcParams.update({
10	    'font.family': 'DejaVu Sans',
11	    'font.size': 9,
12	    'axes.titlesize': 10,
13	    'axes.labelsize': 9,
14	    'xtick.labelsize': 8,
15	    'ytick.labelsize': 8,
16	    'legend.fontsize': 7.5,
17	    'figure.dpi': 300,
18	    'savefig.dpi': 300,
19	    'savefig.bbox': 'tight',
20	    'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,
21	    'axes.spines.top': False,
22	    'axes.spines.right': False,
23	    'pdf.fonttype': 42,
24	    'ps.fonttype': 42,
25	    'lines.linewidth': 1.8,
26	})
27	
28	# Semantic colors (consistent with fig1-3, colorblind-safe Okabe-Ito)
29	C_BASE = '#999999'
30	C_TG   = '#0072B2'
31	C_LORA = '#009E73'
32	C_UG   = '#E69F00'
33	
34	# ── Data ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
35	turns = np.arange(1, 11)
36	
37	# Base: actual per-turn GAR (phase1_gar_diag, 10 turns, 5 conversations)
38	base_data = np.array([0.0418, 0.0241, 0.0224, 0.013, 0.0133,
39	                       0.0086, 0.0078, 0.008, 0.0157, 0.0064])
40	
41	# Summary metrics from analysis_attention_heatmap registry
42	# Base: mean=0.0136, decay=0.875
43	# TG:   mean=0.0232, decay=0.777
44	# UG:   mean=0.0216, decay=0.803
45	# LoRA: mean=0.0216, decay=0.810
46	
47	
48	def exponential_decay_curve(mean_attn, decay_frac, n=10):
49	    """Derive per-turn values from summary statistics.
50	
51	    Model: attn(t) = a1 * exp(-k * (t-1))
52	    k = -ln(1 - decay) / (n - 1)
53	    a1 = mean * n / sum(exp(-k*t) for t in 0..n-1)
54	    """
55	    k = -np.log(1 - decay_frac) / (n - 1)
56	    t = np.arange(n)
57	    weights = np.exp(-k * t)
58	    a1 = mean_attn * n / weights.sum()
59	    return a1 * weights
60	
61	
62	tg_curve = exponential_decay_curve(0.0232, 0.777)
63	ug_curve = exponential_decay_curve(0.0216, 0.803)
64	lora_curve = exponential_decay_curve(0.0216, 0.810)
65	
66	# ── Figure (16:9 double-column) ──────────────────────────────────────
67	fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7, 3.94))
68	
69	# Base (actual data, circles, thinner line for "reference" feel)
70	ax.plot(turns, base_data, 'o-', color=C_BASE, markersize=5,
71	        markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.6,
72	        label='Base (decay 87.5%, μ=.014)', zorder=3, linewidth=1.4)
73	
74	# ThreadGate (squares)
75	ax.plot(turns, tg_curve, 's-', color=C_TG, markersize=4.5,
76	        markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.6,
77	        label='ThreadGate (decay 77.7%, μ=.023)', zorder=4, linewidth=1.8)
78	
79	# LoRA-only (triangles)
80	ax.plot(turns, lora_curve, '^-', color=C_LORA, markersize=5,
81	        markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.6,
82	        label='LoRA-only (decay 81.0%, μ=.022)', zorder=4, linewidth=1.8)
83	
84	# Ungated (diamonds, slightly muted)
85	ax.plot(turns, ug_curve, 'D-', color=C_UG, markersize=4,
86	        markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.6,
87	        label='Ungated (decay 80.3%, μ=.022)', zorder=3, linewidth=1.5,
88	        alpha=0.85)
89	
90	# Fill between TG and LoRA to highlight near-overlap
91	ax.fill_between(turns, tg_curve, lora_curve,
92	                alpha=0.10, color='#555555', zorder=1)
93	
94	# ── Annotations ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
95	
96	# Arrow + label: TG ≈ LoRA overlap
97	mid_idx = 4  # turn 5
98	mid_y = (tg_curve[mid_idx] + lora_curve[mid_idx]) / 2
99	ax.annotate('TG ≈ LoRA-only\n(Δdecay = 3.3pp)',
100	            xy=(turns[mid_idx], mid_y),
101	            xytext=(turns[mid_idx] + 2.2, mid_y + 0.010),
102	            fontsize=7.5, color='#555555', fontstyle='italic', ha='left',
103	            arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color='#999999', lw=0.8,
104	                            connectionstyle='arc3,rad=-0.15'))
105	
106	# Insight box (bottom-right): the key takeaway
107	insight_text = ('LoRA fine-tuning alone explains\n'
108	                'all attention pattern changes.\n'
109	                'Gating: zero incremental effect.')
110	t_ins = ax.text(0.98, 0.35, insight_text, transform=ax.transAxes,
111	                fontsize=7.5, color='#555555', ha='right', va='top',
112	                fontstyle='italic',
113	                bbox=dict(boxstyle='round,pad=0.4', facecolor='#f0f7fb',
114	                          edgecolor='#0072B2', alpha=0.85, linewidth=0.8))
115	
116	# Direct curve labels at turn 10 (right edge) for Base vs cluster
117	for name, y_val, color in [('Base', base_data[-1], C_BASE),
118	                            ('TG', tg_curve[-1], C_TG),
119	                            ('LoRA', lora_curve[-1], C_LORA)]:
120	    t_lbl = ax.text(10.15, y_val, name, fontsize=7, color=color,
121	                    va='center', fontweight='bold')
122	    t_lbl.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])
123	
124	# ── Axes ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
125	ax.set_xticks(turns)
126	ax.set_xlabel('Turn Index')
127	ax.set_ylabel('Goal Attention Rate (GAR)')
128	ax.set_xlim(0.5, 10.8)
129	ax.set_ylim(-0.002, 0.058)
130	
131	ax.set_title('Fine-tuned models share identical decay — gating adds zero overhead',
132	             fontsize=9.5, pad=8, loc='left', fontweight='bold')
133	
134	ax.legend(loc='upper right', framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='#cccccc',
135	          borderpad=0.4, handlelength=1.5, handletextpad=0.4)
136	
137	ax.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.15, zorder=0)
138	
139	# ── Save ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
140	out_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
141	fig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig4_attention_heatmap.pdf'))
142	fig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig4_attention_heatmap.png'))
143	plt.close()
144	print('Saved: fig4_attention_heatmap.pdf + .png')
145	
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Let me review Figure 4 carefully.

**Data Verification:**

Base per-turn data from `phase1_gar_diag.metrics.per_turn_avg_gar`:
```
[0.0418, 0.0241, 0.0224, 0.013, 0.0133, 0.0086, 0.0078, 0.008, 0.0157, 0.0064]
```

Figure 4 code (line 38-39):


Confirming the base data array matches what's in the code. Now I'm checking the summary metrics from the attention heatmap analysis—the baseline shows a mean goal attention of 0.0136 with a decay rate of 0.875, while the two treatment groups have slightly higher attention values at 0.0232 and 0.0216 respectively, with lower decay rates around 0.777 and 0.803.

The LoRA variant has the same mean goal attention as the UG group at 0.0216 but with a slightly higher decay rate of 0.810. These metrics are being used to generate the exponential decay curves that appear in Figure 4, with each method getting its own curve parameterized by these values.

Now I'm noticing a significant methodological issue: while the base model draws from actual per-turn data points collected during the attention analysis, the TG, UG, and LoRA curves are synthetically generated from just two summary statistics. This smooths over real variations that exist in the actual data—like the bump at turn 9 in the base model—and the apparent overlap between TG and LoRA might just be an artifact of how the exponential fitting works rather than reflecting genuine similarity.

I need to verify whether the actual per-turn measurements for all four models are available in the attention heatmap analysis logs, since the analysis did run across all models with the same conversation set.

Looking at the registry metrics, I can see the summary statistics were computed for each model—base, TG, UG, and LoRA—with their respective mean attention and decay values. The issue is that the figure uses synthetic exponential curves fitted from these summary stats rather than the raw per-turn data points that should exist from the analysis. This is a meaningful shortcut that reviewers could legitimately question, especially since the underlying data was already collected and analyzed.

The title overstates the findings—calling the decay rates "identical" when they actually range from 77.7% to 81.0% (a 3.3 percentage point spread) is inaccurate. "Near-identical" would be more honest. The arithmetic checks out, the legend is clear, font sizes meet the minimum threshold, but there's a terminology inconsistency with the y-axis label that needs alignment with how GAR is defined elsewhere in the paper.

Looking at the underlying data, the base model uses actual observations from the phase1_gar_diag runs across 5 conversations and 10 turns, which shows real variation like the spike at turn 9. The other curves rely on synthetic data fitted to exponential models, creating a visual mismatch where the base appears jagged while the others are perfectly smooth. This asymmetry is misleading—I should pull the actual per-turn data from the analysis logs instead of relying on the synthetic curves.
````

_source line 1815 · 2026-05-19T21:02:08.079Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The JSON files contain the full per-turn per-layer attention heatmap data: `avg_heatmap` is a 2D array [layer × turn]. The actual per-turn GAR values can be computed by averaging across layers for each turn position. This data is available and should have been used instead of synthetic exponential curves.

Let me also check:
1. The JSON says `num_conversations: 3` for TG_v7 and LoRA-only, but the registry says `data_count: 5`. Phase1 had 5 conversations, but the attention heatmap analysis may have only used 3. Let me verify...


2. There's a mismatch here — the base phase1_gar_diag used all 5 conversations with 10 turns each, but the heatmap JSONs only show 3 conversations in their metadata. Either the analysis processed a subset of the data, or the JSON metadata is inaccurate.

3. This means the exponential curves are fitted to different underlying datasets, which the fitting process masks by only using aggregate statistics. I should extract the actual per-turn data from the JSON files instead of relying on the synthetic curves.

4. Looking at the data structure, `avg_heatmap` is a 28×10 matrix representing layer-level attention weights across turns, but I need to clarify what this actually measures relative to GAR, which focuses on goal attention positions rather than raw layer attention.

5. The analysis script computes attention from the last token to goal positions, so `avg_heatmap[layer][turn]` gives me the per-turn per-layer GAR data I need. Averaging across layers for each turn would give the true per-turn GAR values.

6. The worker took a shortcut by fitting exponential curves to summary statistics instead of using the actual per-turn data available in the JSON files, which risks obscuring patterns like the non-monotonic spike at turn 9 that might appear across other model variants too.

7. If TG and LoRA don't actually overlap at every individual turn but just happen to have similar means and decay rates, the exponential fitting would mask that discrepancy and overstate their similarity.

8. There's also a sample size mismatch—the heatmap JSON has 3 conversations while phase1 has 5, which could skew the reliability of the comparison.

This warrants flagging as a warning.
```

_source line 1821 · 2026-05-19T21:02:26.303Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
All 4 heatmap JSONs have `num_conversations: 3`. But the Base data in Figure 4 comes from `phase1_gar_diag` which used 5 conversations. So:
- Base curve: from phase1 (5 conversations, 10 turns)
- TG/UG/LoRA curves: synthesized from attention_heatmap analysis (3 conversations, 10 turns) summary stats

This means:
1. Base uses different data source (phase1) vs TG/UG/LoRA (attention_heatmap analysis)
2. Base uses actual per-turn values, TG/UG/LoRA use exponential fits of summary stats


3. Sample sizes differ (5 vs 3 conversations)

There's a deeper issue here — the attention_heatmap analysis also ran the base model with 3 conversations, but the figure uses the phase1 base data instead. This creates a confound where the base is measured differently from the other curves. And phase1 and attention_heatmap likely used different prompts and conversation sets entirely, with phase1 using the built-in five conversations like knowledge_qa, coding_help, creative_writing, math_reasoning, and roleplay_advisor.

So I'm seeing three main issues to flag in the review: the TG/UG/LoRA curves are synthetic, fitted from just two summary statistics when actual per-turn data exists in the heatmap files. The base model uses a different dataset (5 conversations from phase1) than the other curves (3 conversations from attention_heatmap), and they may not even be the same conversation prompts. And there's a discrepancy where all the heatmap JSONs report 3 conversations but the registry mentions 5 conversations with 10 turns each.
```
